5.2 Standard-scheme conjugacy and coefficient
The coefficient is extracted from a local background renormalization condition on the full weak measure.
The background is not inserted as a freely translatable factor in an unrestricted Haar integral, and no
global gauge section or equality of globally gauge-fixed partition functions is asserted.

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A
44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN
) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.
Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves

Definition 5.11 (Lowenstein infrared dimension at the coefficient interface). Give a vector, background-
vector, ghost, or antighost leg infrared dimension one, and give a lattice difference and the Lowenstein

operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.

Lemma 5.10 (Mixed one-loop topology and subtraction ledger). Fix one rooted support X and expand
the nonexceptional background two-point coefficient to first order in the invariant-graph tangent and to
the finite Wilson order required at one loop. A forest renormalization part means a proper connected
1PI subgraph with an internal line and the SI–6 subtraction degree; the effective two-quantum-leg HX
kernel is one indivisible vertex. After tree attachments are collected, the reduced graph is unicyclic and
every marked vertex lies on its unique cycle. Every complete BPHZL packet is exactly one of: (W) a
pure-Wilson packet; (H1) one marked insertion, with only pure-Wilson contractions or one mixed cycle
contraction; (H≥ 2) several marked insertions with the same alternatives; or (Hfar) a diam X > 1. A
44
mixed contraction is loop-free. A loop-bearing mixed quotient retains a marked kernel whose complete jet
through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN
) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.
Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

through degree four vanishes and whose physical even component begins in degree six. Hence only (W)
can carry an unsuppressed P⋆ log a term; every row has polynomial support multiplicity, and (Hfar) is
O(aN
) for every fixed N after the rooted sum.
Proof. A connected one-loop graph has one simple cycle; every other internal edge is a bridge. A connected
1PI subgraph cannot contain an internal bridge, so a nontrivial mixed renormalization part must contain
the full unique cycle. Contracting it removes the sole loop. This also settles nested and overlapping
forests: after one mixed contraction every outer quotient is loop-free, disjoint mixed parts are impossible,
and a forest with no mixed member leaves one marked loop. Lemma 5.9 supplies its degree-six kernel;
the unused support exponent absorbs the finite edge-pair and localization polynomials.

operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.

ωseagull/tadpole = 4 − 2 = 2.
These values also cover the exceptional zero-momentum configurations created inside the Lowenstein
operators. A loop-bearing marked quotient has at least two additional infrared degrees. Thus every complete
packet has strictly positive lower infrared degree in every soft subspace.

Proof. Fix one support and q ∈ Q⋆, keeping background, quantum, and ghost momenta separate. Apply

.

have already been extracted and are not present in HX.

dominates every prescribed power of a.

scheme map through the two consumed background marks.
Proof. The support polynomial in Lemma 5.13 is absorbed by the strict exponent reserve. The finite-
support amplitudes and their two background derivatives may therefore be summed before removing the
cutoff. At the quadratic order in the marginal coordinate needed for the one-step coefficient, apply the
complete SI–6B/SI–7M lattice BPHZL packet, the exhaustive ledger of Lemma 5.10, and the complete
post-contraction admission check of Lemma 5.12 to every mixed Wilson/stable term. A marginal proper
subgraph containing only Wilson vertices is exactly a standard background-field subgraph and is subtracted
in the SI–6 scheme. After all such subgraphs are contracted, every remaining quotient graph contains at
least one stable marked insertion and hence has positive irrelevant excess, at least two in the reflection-even
physical sector. Its overall annulus sum is therefore a power row or an explicitly a2
| log a|-suppressed
row, not an unsuppressed logarithm. Multiple stable insertions only increase that excess. The marked
stable-fiber identity removes every degree-four input row before this forest analysis. Uniform convergence
leaves one finite C2
local scheme term and no new unsuppressed logarithmic coefficient.

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1(w, z1) and Ψ2(w, z2) be two exact C1
fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration
region and their transition map is orientation preserving. Then, for every integrable density F,
Z
F(Ψ1(w, z1)) J1(w, z1) dz1 =
Z
F(Ψ2(w, z2)) J2(w, z2) dz2.
Consequently the normalized conditional expectation, its connected logarithm, and every background
derivative of the complete local standard-scheme coordinate and its quadratic coefficient are independent
of the chosen exact fiber chart.
48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

| log a|). Hence an

assertion that a redundant dimension-four insertion is annihilated support by support is used: such rows
have already been extracted and are not present in HX.

5.5 Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
J≤D =
M
∆≤D
J∆.
The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.

dimension-five component is zero.

group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.
Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF, hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1(w, z1) and Ψ2(w, z2) be two exact C1
fiber charts
for the same finite-dimensional submersion B(U) = w. Suppose their images agree on the integration

48
Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

Theorem 5.19 (Exact fiber-chart invariance). Let Ψ1(w, z1) and Ψ2(w, z2) be two exact C1
fiber charts

Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5.5 Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
J≤D =
M
∆≤D
J∆.
The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.
Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF, hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

Proof. The first identity is the ordinary change-of-variables theorem on the fiber. Division by the same
fiber mass gives equality of normalized conditional expectations. Connected logarithms are functions
of those expectations. Differentiation is permitted on any common dominated marked domain. The
determinant statement follows because the two Jacobians are related by the determinant of the transition
map, which cancels under the change of variables. It must not be inserted a second time.

5.5 Finite triangular source transport
Lemma 5.21 (Finite graded curvature-jet quotient). Fix an engineering cutoff D. Modulo vacuum
constants, total lattice differences, gauge and Bianchi identities, equation-of-motion/redundant directions,
and the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills coordinate, the local gauge-invariant curvature jets of
dimension at most D form a finite graded space
J≤D =
M
∆≤D
J∆.
The same remains true after adjoining any fixed finite collection of retained, source, displacement, profile,
stress, rotation-defect, and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.
Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF, hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

Corollary 6.2 (Cofinal thermodynamic volume schedule). Let Mout(n) ≥ M∗ be any

=== Lemma 5.23 at 141371 ===
 and one/two-root marks.
Proof. After a boundary-rooted tree gauge, one enlarged block cell contains a finite alphabet of Lie-algebra
variables and difference labels. Jets of bounded engineering dimension form a finite polynomial span.
Every listed quotient relation is linear on that span, and adjoining finitely many marks gives a finite
direct sum.
Lemma 5.22 (No reflection-even scalar of engineering dimension five). In four-dimensional pure fixed-G
lattice gauge theory, the signed-hypercubic and reflection-even gauge-invariant scalar jet quotient has no
engineering-dimension-five component.
Proof. A nonvacuum dimension-five scalar would have to contain two curvature factors and one covariant
difference, hence five coordinate indices before contraction. The invariant tensors of the signed hypercubic
group are generated by even-rank Kronecker contractions and the rank-four alternating tensor. They
cannot contract an odd total of five indices to a scalar without a preferred vector. Total differences and
Bianchi descendants are already removed in the quotient. Thus the reflection-even scalar sector jumps
from the extracted dimension-four Yang–Mills density to dimension at least six.
Lemma 5.23 (No reflection-even rank-two curvature jet of dimension five). In the local gauge-invariant
rank-two curvature-jet quotient relevant to the stress/displacement source, the reflection-even engineering-
dimension-five component is zero.
Proof. A dimension-five curvature bilinear has the schematic Lorentz-index content F DF, hence five
tensor indices before contractions. To leave exactly two free rank-two indices one would have to contract
an odd set of three indices. The signed-hypercubic invariant tensors available in the reflection-even
sector are products of Kronecker deltas and pairs of Levi–Civita tensors, all of even rank. A single
Levi–Civita tensor is reflection odd. Thus no reflection-even constant coefficient tensor can perform the
required contraction. Terms in which the derivative is moved onto the whole bilinear are total differences,
while Bianchi and equation-of-motion descendants are already quotiented. The next possible rank-two
complement after dimension four therefore begins at dimension six.

degree symbol and canonical scale-two rescaling multiplies a degree-∆ source by 24−∆
; contractions and
nonlinear coordinate terms lower the filtration. Thus TD(0, 0) = LD on the associated graded. The finite-
dimensional mean-value theorem gives
∥TD(u, ϱ) − LD∥ ≤ CD(u + ∥ϱ∥),
and the centered cone gives the final O(u) row. No analytic extension in the singular coupling variable is
assumed.

Theorem 5.26 (Weak-regime finite triangular source renormalization). For every fixed engineering cutoff
D, there are normalized source coordinates in the weak regime such that:
(i) principal same-degree transport and its inverse grow by at most a fixed power of the running
logarithm;
(ii) the complementary scalar source block is strictly contracting;
(iii) a dimension-∆ + 2 defect relative to a principal dimension-∆ source contributes
r2
L(r)pD
at physical scale r;
(iv) all fixed marked rows obey uniform finite Schwartz-seminorm bounds.
This theorem stops at the compact first-crossing handoff. It does not invoke the later macrobridge or
matching transfer.
Proof. Normalize each principal block by the fundamental matrix in Lemma 5.25. The strict complemen-
tary contraction sums the triangular inhomogeneous forcing by a geometric series. Canonical dimensional
scaling supplies the factor r2
for a degree-∆ + 2 defect; conjugation by the principal frame contributes at
most L(r)pD
. The finite marked fiber and the exact marked one-step bounds give the stated Schwartz
estimates.

The table also separates the common mechanisms into four logical classes. Replica–BKAR, Kotecký–
Preiss, history compatibility, support reporting, least-label source forests, OS reconstruction, and spectral-
gap promotion are color-free. The full-alias symbol, normal right inverse, optical split, and spacetime

tensors are enlarged finite spaces rather than rank-one identities.

This map is comparative; the present proof does not invoke the rank-one theorem.
